# Nevermore Package Information

This file is the machine-readable package catalog for AI assistants working in this repository. Read the package directory first, use tags to narrow a search, then inspect the matching package card and its submodule table. Do not infer a package's location from its display name: the **Path** field is authoritative and points into the versioned [`src/_Index/`](src/_Index/) implementation tree.

Each package card is intentionally regular:

- **Purpose** is the decision-level reason to use the package.
- **Path** is the exact repository directory.
- **Short Description** summarizes behavior and public responsibility.
- **Tags** are searchable domain keywords; packages may have several.

The submodule table gives the exact file path, realm, module kind, and public responsibility for every production module. Use those paths when opening source files. Test specifications and tooling files are intentionally excluded.

**Coverage:** 219 packages · 902 production modules

Every package below uses the same quick-reference card: purpose, source path, a short human-readable description, and searchable tags. The submodule table follows when a package has more than one public implementation detail.

## AI lookup rules

1. Treat the package heading and **Path** as the canonical package record.
2. Treat paths in the submodule table as exact, case-sensitive file paths.
3. Import another package through its flat `@game/ReplicatedStorage/Packages/<Export>` wrapper. Use relative paths only within the same indexed package.
4. Check the **Runtime** column in a submodule row before suggesting server-only or client-only usage.
5. Read the implementation and README when the catalog's short description is not enough to answer a behavior question.

# acceltween

- **Purpose:** Smooth motion with an explicit acceleration limit.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_acceltween@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_acceltween@0.0.1/)

# access

- **Purpose:** A shared fact, feature, and policy system for gating game access.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Centralizes game-access decisions. Facts contribute evidence, policies evaluate that evidence, and replicated state lets client UI and server authority agree on the same feature gates.
- **Tags:** `animation`, `commerce`, `data`, `developer_tools`, `enums`, `game_service`, `gui`, `lifecycle`, `localization`, `math`, `player_service`, `reactive`, `security`, `serialization`, `utility`

Facts the client genuinely cannot resolve are the exception, and those replicate.                                                                                         |

| [`Shared/AccessFactContributionState.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessFactContributionState.luau)                                     | shared | Class/model | What a fact layer said, named rather than encoded in a boolean and a nil. `nil` used to carry two of these at once, which is the root of a whole family of bugs: a Lua table cannot hold nil, so an unresolved fact simply vanished from any map it was put in, and _ **ALLOW** -- yes. _ **DENY** -- no. _ **UNRESOLVED** -- I tried and cannot say yet. An answer, not a silence. _ **ABSTAIN** -- not my question.          |
| [`Shared/AccessFactContributionStateUtils.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessFactContributionStateUtils.luau)                           | shared | Class/model | Moving between an [AccessFactContributionState] and the plain values authors write. Authoring stays cheap -- a resolver may still return `true`, `false`, or an observable of `boolean?` and the boundary names what arrived. What is no longer possible is an unlabelled nil reaching the merge, where it used to mean two different things depending on where you looked.                                                    |
| [`Shared/AccessFactNames.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessFactNames.luau)                                                             | shared | Class/model | The fact names the package itself registers. A fact name is API: it is what a spec overrides, what someone types into the console, and what a feature declares. Naming the ones this package ships means a game, a downstream package, and a console command all spell them the same way without having to coordinate.                                                                                                         |
| [`Shared/AccessFactPriority.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessFactPriority.luau)                                                       | shared | Class/model | Where a fact layer sits when several answer the same fact. Rather than let registration order decide, every layer declares a priority and the highest one that has something to say wins.                                                           |
| [`Shared/AccessFactServerOverrideBehavior.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessFactServerOverrideBehavior.luau)                           | shared | Class/model | How a fact's server answer combines with the one the client worked out for itself. The server's answer **always replicates** -- that part is not configurable, because a client that silently never hears is the failure nobody can debug. What a fact chooses is what the client should _do_ with it, and there are only four sensible answers, so they are named rather than left to a predicate somebody has to read.       |
| [`Shared/AccessFeature.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessFeature.luau)                                                                 | shared | Class/model | One capability a game gates: entering a chapter, buying an egg, opening a demo area. A feature is policy -- it reads facts and **decides**. Every judgement lives here: which combination of facts opens a thing, what a release flag means for it, inversions, per-thing context. Facts state what is true; features decide what that is worth. That is why the same fact can grant one feature and deny another.             |
| [`Shared/AccessPlayerInterface.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessPlayerInterface.luau)                                                 | shared | Class/model | The tie for [AccessPlayerBase], so anything holding a player can ask about their access without requiring the access package or plumbing a [ServiceBag] to the call site. Registering it does not take ownership. A policy declares what it reads, the same way a feature does, and its context only hands back what it declared.                         |
| [`Shared/AccessPolicyContextUtils.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessPolicyContextUtils.luau)                                           | shared | Class/model | The thing an [AccessPolicy] is handed when it runs. | [`Shared/AccessPolicyRealm.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessPolicyRealm.luau)                                                         | shared | Class/model | Which realm a policy's consequence runs in. Policies are registered in **both** realms regardless, so a console on either side knows every policy name and can autocomplete it. The realm decides only where `apply` actually runs -- kicking is server-side, showing a piece of UI is client-side, and neither should half-happen on the wrong one. Public entry points include `AccessPolicyRealm.runsHere`.                 |
| [`Shared/AccessPolicyService.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessPolicyService.luau)                                                     | shared | Service     | Registers [AccessPolicy] objects and runs the enabled ones against each player. Policies register **disabled** unless they asked not to. A policy that is off is still listed, still autocompletes, and still names what it reads -- it simply is not running.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [`Shared/AccessPolicyServiceInterface.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/AccessPolicyServiceInterface.luau)                                   | shared | Class/model | The tie for [AccessPolicyService], so a console pane or a debug UI can list policies and see what is running without depending on this package. Bind them at the registration site, where both halves read in a line: GOTCHA, and the reason this is a class rather than a pattern to copy: **a kick never fires on an unanswered input.** "We could not find out whether you… |
| [`Shared/JSONAttributeValue.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/JSONAttributeValue.luau)                                                       | shared | Class/model | Stores a JSON-serializable value in one Roblox attribute and exposes it as an observable value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [`Shared/Replication/AccessReplicationState.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/Replication/AccessReplicationState.luau)                       | shared | Class/model | What this realm has been told about a fact, as four distinct states rather than a boolean and a guess. Helpers live in [AccessReplicationStateUtils]. Three of these look like "false" if you flatten them, and each needs a different response: _ **NOT_YET_ARRIVED** -- the server has not spoken. Never overrides; the local answer stands. _ **ABSTAINED** -- the server has no layer for this fact either.                |
| [`Shared/Replication/AccessReplicationStateUtils.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/Replication/AccessReplicationStateUtils.luau)             | shared | Class/model | Reading an [AccessReplicationState] out of what actually arrived, and asking the one question the combine rules turn on. Public entry points include `AccessReplicationStateUtils.fromEntry`, `AccessReplicationStateUtils.hasAnswer`, `AccessReplicationStateUtils.isReplicationState`.                                                                                                                                       |
| [`Shared/RxAccessStateUtils.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_access@0.0.1/Shared/RxAccessStateUtils.luau)                                                       | shared | Class/model | Observable plumbing for access. The tri-state -- `true                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | false | nil`-- has to travel through observables like any other value, and none of the obvious [Rx] operators carry`nil`: * `Rx.startWith({nil})`iterates its list, and a Lua list cannot hold nil, so it fires nothing. *`Rx.defaultsTo(nil)`only fires on fail or complete, so a lookup still in flight emits nothing at all. *`Rx.of(nil)`relies on varargs packing surviving a lone nil, and completes besides. That matters because [Rx.combineLatest] waits for every source to emit once. A fact that stays silent until its lookup lands would keep the whole feature from emitting -- so a live consumer renders nothing instead of rendering "not yet known". Public entry points include`RxAccessStateUtils.ofStatic`, `RxAccessStateUtils.startUnresolved`, `RxAccessStateUtils.unresolvedOnError`, `RxAccessStateUtils.distinctState`, `RxAccessStateUtils.completeOn`, `finish`. |

Shipping a name means every game gates the same capability by the same string, and a package that wants to widen it has something to widen. See [AccessFeature.PushFactAllowsFeature].                                                                                                                                                                                         |

# adorneedata

- **Purpose:** Bridges attributes and serializable data table. It's typical to need to define data in 3 ways. 1. Attributes on an instance for replication 2. Tables for Lua configuration 3. Within AttributeValues for writing regular code Providing all 3 Here's how the usage works: You can then use the data to retrieve values

# basicpane

- **Purpose:** Base UI object with visibility and a maid. BasicPane provides three points of utility. 1. BasicPane contain visibility API. It's very standard practice to use the VisibleChanged event and pass visibility up or down the entire stack. 2. BasicPane contains a maid which cleans up upon :Destroy(). This just saves some time typing. 3. Finally, BasicPanes, by convention (although not requirement), contain a .Gui object which can generally be safely reparented to another object.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_basicpane@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_basicpane@0.0.1/)

It's very standard practice to use the VisibleChanged event and pass visibility up or down the entire stack. 2. BasicPane contains a maid which cleans up upon :Destroy(). This just saves some time typing. 3. Finally, BasicPanes, by convention (although not requirement), contain a .Gui object which can generally be safely reparented to another object.
- **Tags:** `gui`, `lifecycle`

# brio

- **Purpose:** Brios wrap a value (or tuple of values) and are used to convey the lifetime of that object. The brio is better than a maid, by providing the following constraints: - Can be in 2 states, dead or alive. - While alive, can retrieve values. - While dead, retrieving values is forbidden. - Died will fire once upon death. Brios encapsulate the "lifetime" of a valid resource. Unlike a maid, they - Can only die once, ensuring duplicate calls never occur. - Have less memory leaks. Memory leaks in maids can occur when use of the maid occurs after the cleanup of the maid has occured, in certain race conditions. - Cannot be reentered, i.e. cannot retrieve values after death. Calling `brio:Destroy()` or `brio:Kill()` after death does nothing. Brios cannot be resurrected. Brios are useful for downstream events where you want to emit a resource. Typically brios should be killed when their source is killed. Brios are intended to be merged with downstream brios so create a chain of reliable resources. Brios are designed to solve this issue where we emit an object with a lifetime associated with it from an Observable stream. This resource is only valid for some amount of time (for example, while the object is in the Roblox data model). In order to know how long we can keep this object/use it, we wrap the object with a Brio, which denotes the lifetime of the object. Modeling this with pure observables is very tricky because the subscriber will have to also monitor/emit a similar object with less clear conventions. For example an observable that emits the object, and then nil on death.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_brio@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_brio@0.0.1/)

Brios encapsulate the "lifetime" of a valid resource. Unlike a maid, they - Can only die once, ensuring duplicate calls never occur. - Have less memory leaks. Memory leaks in maids can occur when use of the maid occurs after the cleanup of the maid has occured, in certain race conditions. - Cannot be reentered, i.e. cannot retrieve values after death. Calling `brio:Destroy()` or `brio:Kill()` after death does nothing. Brios cannot be resurrected. Brios are useful for downstream events where you want to emit a resource. Typically brios should be killed when their source is killed. Brios are intended to be merged with downstream brios so create a chain of reliable resources. Brios are designed to solve this issue where we emit an object with a lifetime associated with it from an Observable stream. This resource is only valid for some amount of time (for example, while the object is in the Roblox data model). In order to know how long we can keep this object/use it, we wrap the object with a Brio, which denotes the lifetime of the object. Modeling this with pure observables is very tricky because the subscriber will have to also monitor/emit a similar object with less clear conventions. For example an observable that emits the object, and then nil on death.

# cmdrservice

- **Purpose:** Registers and secures Cmdr commands. Table-defined commands are converted into the same paired ModuleScripts Cmdr normally expects. A shared definition module is replicated by Cmdr for parsing and autocomplete, while a server execution module forwards back into the function retained by this service.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_cmdrservice@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_cmdrservice@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Registers and secures Cmdr commands. Table-defined commands are converted into the same paired ModuleScripts Cmdr normally expects. A shared definition module is replicated by Cmdr for parsing and autocomplete, while a server execution module forwards back into the function retained by this service.
- **Tags:** `developer_tools`, `enums`, `game_service`, `utility`

# colorpicker

- **Purpose:** `ColorPicker` provides client-side HSV color controls built with Blend. The package creates its own GUI, supports mouse and touch dragging, exposes the selected color as both `Color3` and HSV, and automatically chooses contrasting cursor colors.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_colorpicker@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_colorpicker@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** `ColorPicker` provides client-side HSV color controls built with Blend. The package creates its own GUI, supports mouse and touch dragging, exposes the selected color as both `Color3` and HSV, and automatically chooses contrasting cursor colors.
- **Tags:** `data`, `gui`, `input`, `lifecycle`, `math`, `player_service`, `reactive`, `security`, `serialization`, `utility`

# conditions

- **Purpose:** Utility library that defines a generalized interface for scriptable conditions. These conditions are rooted in [Rx] and can be scripted by a variety of systems. For example, we may have conditions on what ammo we can consume, or whether or not an action can be activated. This library provides an interface for this to happen in. Conditions are marshalled through a BindableFunction and must be defined on both the client and server. However, these conditions can be reactive. It would be very simple to create a condition that also just links to a bool value if conditions should be only on the server.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_conditions@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_conditions@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Utility library that defines a generalized interface for scriptable conditions. These conditions are rooted in [Rx] and can be scripted by a variety of systems. For example, we may have conditions on what ammo we can consume, or whether or not an action can be activated. This library provides an interface for this to happen in. Conditions are marshalled through a BindableFunction and must be defined on both the client and server. However, these conditions can be reactive. It would be very simple to create a condition that also just links to a bool value if conditions should be only on the server.
- **Tags:** `reactive`, `utility`

### Submodules

| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

These conditions are rooted in [Rx] and can be scripted by a variety of systems. For example, we may have conditions on what ammo we can consume, or whether or not an action can be activated. This library provides an interface for this to happen in. |

# cull

- **Purpose:** `ClientCullingService` performs render-only distance culling for tagged world content on each client. It keeps tagged Instances in their original hierarchy, so culling does not change ancestry, collisions, raycasts, scripts, or Roblox streaming behavior.

- **Short Description:** `ClientCullingService` performs render-only distance culling for tagged world content on each client. It keeps tagged Instances in their original hierarchy, so culling does not change ancestry, collisions, raycasts, scripts, or Roblox streaming behavior.

# draw

- **Purpose:** Debug drawing library useful for debugging 3D abstractions. One of the more useful utility libraries. These functions are incredibly easy to invoke for quick debugging. This can make debugging any sort of 3D geometry really easy. This library should not be used to render things in production for normal players, as it is optimized for debug experience over performance.

# ellipticcurvecryptography

- **Purpose:** Elliptic curve arithmetic Field Size: 168 bits Field Modulus (p): 481 _ 2^159 + 3 Equation: x^2 + y^2 = 1 + 122 _ x^2 _ y^2 Parameters: Edwards Curve with d = 122 Curve Order (n): 351491143778082151827986174289773107581916088585564 Cofactor (h): 4 Generator Order (q): 87872785944520537956996543572443276895479022146391 Current best attack security: 81.777 bits (Small Subgroup + Rho) Rho Security: log2(0.884 _ sqrt(q)) = 82.777 bits Transfer Security? Yes: p ~= q; k > 20 Field Discriminant Security? Yes: t = 27978492958645335688000168 s = 10 |D| = 6231685068753619775430107799412237267322159383147 > 2^100 Rigidity? No, not at all. XZ/YZ Ladder Security? No: Single coordinate ladders are insecure. Small Subgroup Security? No. Invalid Curve Security? Yes: Points are checked before every operation. Invalid Curve Twist Security? No: Don't use single coordinate ladders. Completeness? Yes: The curve is complete. Indistinguishability? Yes (Elligator 2), but not implemented.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_ellipticcurvecryptography@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_ellipticcurvecryptography@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Elliptic curve arithmetic Field Size: 168 bits Field Modulus (p): 481 _ 2^159 + 3 Equation: x^2 + y^2 = 1 + 122 _ x^2 _ y^2 Parameters: Edwards Curve with d = 122 Curve Order (n): 351491143778082151827986174289773107581916088585564 Cofactor (h): 4 Generator Order (q): 87872785944520537956996543572443276895479022146391 Current best attack security: 81.777 bits (Small Subgroup + Rho) Rho Security: log2(0.884 _ sqrt(q)) = 82.777 bits Transfer Security? Yes: p ~= q; k > 20 Field Discriminant Security? Yes: t = 27978492958645335688000168 s = 10 |D| = 6231685068753619775430107799412237267322159383147 > 2^100 Rigidity? No, not at all. XZ/YZ Ladder Security? No: Single coordinate ladders are insecure. Small Subgroup Security? No. Invalid Curve Security? Yes: Points are checked before every operation. Invalid Curve Twist Security? No: Don't use single coordinate ladders. Completeness? Yes: The curve is complete. Indistinguishability? Yes (Elligator 2), but not implemented.

# fakeskybox

- **Purpose:** `FakeSkybox` renders a client-side sky from six camera-centered parts, plus separate sun and moon parts. Unlike a normal `Sky`, it can spring-fade in and out, which makes it useful for transitions, local sky overrides, cutscenes, and preview cameras.

# fzy

- **Purpose:** The lua implementation of the fzy string matching algorithm. This algorithm is optimized for matching stuff on the terminal, but should serve well as a baseline search algorithm within a game too. See: _ https://github.com/swarn/fzy-lua _ https://github.com/jhawthorn/fzy/blob/master/ALGORITHM.md Modified from the initial code to fit this codebase. While this definitely messes with some naming which may have been better, it also keeps usage of this library consistent with other libraries. Notes: _ A higher score is better than a lower score _ Scoring time is `O(n*m)` where `n` is the length of the needle and `m` is the length of the haystack. * Scoring memory is also `O(n*m)` \* Should do quite well with small lists
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_fzy@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_fzy@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** The lua implementation of the fzy string matching algorithm. This algorithm is optimized for matching stuff on the terminal, but should serve well as a baseline search algorithm within a game too. See: _ https://github.com/swarn/fzy-lua _ https://github.com/jhawthorn/fzy/blob/master/ALGORITHM.md Modified from the initial code to fit this codebase. While this definitely messes with some naming which may have been better, it also keeps usage of this library consistent with other libraries. Notes: _ A higher score is better than a lower score _ Scoring time is `O(n*m)` where `n` is the length of the needle and `m` is the length of the haystack. * Scoring memory is also `O(n*m)` \* Should do quite well with small lists
- **Tags:** `localization`

# gifting

- **Purpose:** `GiftingService` stores virtual pass and developer-product entitlements under a player's `Gifted` PlayerDataStoreService root. It uses GameConfig keys, enforces configurable social restrictions, keeps server and client caches, serializes the client projection with Squash, and can fulfill paid gifts through `GameProductService`.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_gifting@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_gifting@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** `GiftingService` stores virtual pass and developer-product entitlements under a player's `Gifted` PlayerDataStoreService root. # highlight

# humanoidkillerutils

- **Purpose:** Tags and retrieves killer. This is the old API surface to register KOs in Roblox, and many legacy systems still use the creator tag. The contract is that an object value pointing to the killer will be put in the humanoid, and if it exists when the humanoid dies, then the killer is registered.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_humanoidkillerutils@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_humanoidkillerutils@0.0.1/)

# playeridservice

- **Purpose:** Assigns every current player a unique replicated `uint8` ID. Automatic IDs use the compact `1..Players.MaxPlayers` range, remain stable for the player's session, and are recycled after departure. Zero is intentionally left available as an unassigned network sentinel.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_playeridservice@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_playeridservice@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Assigns every current player a unique replicated `uint8` ID. Automatic IDs use the compact `1..Players.MaxPlayers` range, remain stable for the player's session, and are recycled after departure. Zero is intentionally left available as an unassigned network sentinel.
- **Tags:** `data`, `enums`, `game_service`, `lifecycle`, `math`, `player_service`, `reactive`, `security`, `serialization`, `utility`

IDs replicate through a Player attribute and can be sent directly through compact network schemas. # playermock

- **Purpose:** `PlayerMock` is an in-memory `Folder` marked with the `PlayerMock` CollectionService tag. It lets tests exercise code that expects a `Player` without trying to construct Roblox's non-constructible `Player` class.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_playermock@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_playermock@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** `PlayerMock` is an in-memory `Folder` marked with the `PlayerMock` CollectionService tag. It lets tests exercise code that expects a `Player` without trying to construct Roblox's non-constructible `Player` class.
- **Tags:** `commerce`, `data`, `game_service`, `math`, `player_service`, `reactive`

### Submodules

| Module                                              | Realm  | Kind        | Responsibility and public surface                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [`PlayerMock.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_playermock@0.0.1/PlayerMock.luau) | shared | Class/model | In-memory stand-in for a Roblox `Player` used by tests. A real `Player` cannot be `Instance.new`'d and no client joins a headless Open Cloud test place, so this builds a `Folder` that carries the marker and identity attributes production code reads -- letting a "player" flow through guards and providers without a real join. |

# random

- **Purpose:** Chooses weighted options while applying a reactive pity value to options with luck influence. Pity increases after non-lucky choices and can reset after a lucky choice. Numeric weights have no luck influence; use a weight record to mark and boost lucky options. The adjusted weight is `weight * (1 + luckInfluence * pity)`.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_random@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_random@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Chooses weighted options while applying a reactive pity value to options with luck influence. Pity increases after non-lucky choices and can reset after a lucky choice. Numeric weights have no luck influence; use a weight record to mark and boost lucky options. The adjusted weight is `weight * (1 + luckInfluence * pity)`.
- **Tags:** `data`, `math`

# randomutils

- **Purpose:** Utility functions involving random variables. This is quite useful for a variety of game mechanics. Each method generally takes a random object in as the last argument, which can be used to seed the randomness. This is especially useful for reproducting state in testing.

# rogueproperty

- **Purpose:** `RogueProperty` is a reactive property system whose final value is computed from a base value plus an ordered list of temporary modifiers. It stores runtime state on Roblox Instances, exposes ValueObject-like access, supports nested tables and homogeneous arrays, and lets unrelated systems add or remove effects without overwriting each other.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_rogueproperty@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_rogueproperty@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** `RogueProperty` is a reactive property system whose final value is computed from a base value plus an ordered list of temporary modifiers. It stores runtime state on Roblox Instances, exposes ValueObject-like access, supports nested tables and homogeneous arrays, and lets unrelated systems add or remove effects without overwriting each other.

# rxbinderutils

- **Purpose:** Utility methods to observe bound objects on instances. This is what makes the Rx library with binders really good. Using this API, you can query most game-state in very efficient ways, and react to the world changing in real-time. This makes programming streaming and other APIs really nice.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_rxbinderutils@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_rxbinderutils@0.0.1/)

# rxbrioutils

- **Purpose:** Utility functions involving brios and rx. Brios encapsulate the lifetime of resources, which could be expired by the time a subscription occurs. These functions allow us to manipulate the state of these at a higher order.

# rxinstanceutils

- **Purpose:** Utility functions to observe the state of Roblox. This is a very powerful way to query Roblox's state. Use RxInstanceUtils to program streaming enabled games, and make it easy to debug. This API surface lets you use Roblox as a source-of-truth which is very valuable.

- **Purpose:** `SaveSlotService` adapts Nevermore's SaveSlot lifecycle to this fork's `PlayerDataStoreService`. It does not open another DataStore or ProfileStore session. Instead, each player's existing profile contains account-wide data plus a `SaveSlots` branch:

# serverlist

- **Purpose:** `ServerListService` publishes a bounded place-scoped directory through `MemoryStoreHashMap`. Each game server publishes one expiring entry, while directory reads happen only when at least one local client observes the list. All interested clients in the same server share one cache and refresh operation.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_serverlist@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_serverlist@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** `ServerListService` publishes a bounded place-scoped directory through `MemoryStoreHashMap`. Each game server publishes one expiring entry, while directory reads happen only when at least one local client observes the list. All interested clients in the same server share one cache and refresh operation.

# simulatedcharacterservice

- **Purpose:** `SimulatedCharacterService` runs lightweight, server-authoritative NPC roots on a flat X/Z plane. The server stores numbers rather than physical character models. Each client clones, animates, interpolates, ragdolls, and culls its own visual models.

# sortedlist

- **Purpose:** A list that can be observed for blend and other components and maintains sorting order. This allows you to observe both an index, observe a value at an index, and more. This class is a red-black binary sorted tree. Unlike previous iterations of this class, we can add values in log(n) time, and remove in log(n) time, and it uses less memory. Previously we'd use O(n^2) processing time when constructing this class. We reuse the node itself as the indexing key. This class always prefers to add equivalent elements to the end of the list if they're not in the list. Otherwise it prefers minimal movement.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_sortedlist@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_sortedlist@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** A list that can be observed for blend and other components and maintains sorting order. This allows you to observe both an index, observe a value at an index, and more. This class is a red-black binary sorted tree. Unlike previous iterations of this class, we can add values in log(n) time, and remove in log(n) time, and it uses less memory. Previously we'd use O(n^2) processing time when constructing this class. We reuse the node itself as the indexing key. This class always prefers to add equivalent elements to the end of the list if they're not in the list. Otherwise it prefers minimal movement.
- **Tags:** `data`, `reactive`

# soundplayer

- **Purpose:** `SoundPlayer` is the client playback layer for long-running music, ambience, and other sounds that need smooth transitions. It can cross-fade between tracks, schedule changes at loop boundaries, choose sounds from a collection, synchronize loops to a clock or BPM, combine multiple synchronized layers, and arbitrate between competing players by priority.

# spring

- **Purpose:** A physical model of a spring, useful in many applications. A spring is an object that will compute based upon Hooke's law. Properties only evaluate upon index making this model good for lazy applications. A good visualization can be found here, provided by Defaultio: https://www.desmos.com/calculator/hn2i9shxbz

# sunpositionutils

- **Purpose:** Utility to position the sun and to retrieve sun information specific to Roblox. Note this is not an accurate guess of where the sun would be on earth, but rather the computation to compute where Roblox is rendering the sun given the lighting properties set. https://raw.githubusercontent.com/iryl1/RBX_DOCUMENTATIONS/c0db57fedf0540db2cd9eb65404ad258c3e21295/lighting.md

# transparency

- **Purpose:** Allows a model to have transparent set locally on the client. Supports all BasePart types including: - Part - MeshPart - UnionOperation - TrussPart - WedgePart - CornerWedgePart Also supports Decals. Can optionally disable visual effects like ParticleEmitters, Lights, Beams, etc.
- **Path:** [`src/_Index/quenty_transparency@0.0.1/`](src/_Index/quenty_transparency@0.0.1/)
- **Short Description:** Allows a model to have transparent set locally on the client. Supports all BasePart types including: - Part - MeshPart - UnionOperation - TrussPart - WedgePart - CornerWedgePart Also supports Decals. Can optionally disable visual effects like ParticleEmitters, Lights, Beams, etc.
- **Tags:** `enums`, `game_service`

| Module                                                                          | Realm  | Kind        | Responsibility and public surface                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [`ModelTransparencyEffect.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_transparency@0.0.1/ModelTransparencyEffect.luau) | client | Class/model | Allows a model to have transparent set locally on the client. Supports all BasePart types including: - Part - MeshPart - UnionOperation - TrussPart - WedgePart - CornerWedgePart Also supports Decals. Can optionally disable visual effects like ParticleEmitters, Lights, Beams, etc.                                                                                                           |
| [`ModelTransparencyEffectUtils.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_transparency@0.0.1/ModelTransparencyEffectUtils.luau) | shared | Utility | Identifies visual-effect instances supported by `ModelTransparencyEffect`. Public entry points include `ModelTransparencyEffectUtils.isVisualEffect`. |
| [`TransparencyService.luau`](src/_Index/quenty_transparency@0.0.1/TransparencyService.luau)         | shared | Service     | Service that orchistrates transparency setting from multiple colliding sources and handle the transparency appropriately. This means that 2 systems can work with transparency without knowing about each other. Public entry points include `TransparencyService.Init`, `TransparencyService.IsDead`, `TransparencyService.SetTransparency`, `TransparencyService.SetLocalTransparencyModifier`,… |
